## Decision Tree Depth and Communication Complexity via Lifting

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: DECISION_TREE_DEPTH × COMMUNICATION_COMPLEXITY
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 00:11 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `eeec3f948dcf`

### Statement

For any Boolean function f on n variables, if the decision tree depth of f is d, then the deterministic communication complexity R_{1/3}(f) is Ω(d / log n).

### Rationale

This conjecture explores the relationship between the depth of a decision tree and the communication complexity of the function, leveraging lifting techniques to establish a lower bound. If true, it would imply that functions with deeper decision trees require more communication, which is a key insight in understanding the interplay between these complexity measures.

## Laplacian Apolar Rank Caps Delorme-Poljak Max-CUT SoS-2 Gap

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: Curto–Fialkow real moment problem / apolarity & Hankel flat-extension theory (real algebraic geometry). The Hankel rank of the Laplacian moment sequence m_k(G) := tr(L_G^k) is by the Hamburger truncated moment theorem the smallest support size of an atomic measure on R_{≥0} reproducing the moments — equivalently the number of distinct Laplacian eigenvalues (Curto–Fialkow 1996; Lasserre 2009 'Moments, Positive Polynomials and their Applications'; Blekherman–Parrilo–Thomas 2012 'Semidefinite Optimization and Convex Algebraic Geometry'; Reznick 1992). The invariant uses real-eigenvalue clustering and rank-of-Hankel — non-ring (no F_q polynomial extension preserves cluster count of real eigenvalues under polynomial ring lifts), Aaronson–Wigderson algebrization-safe. An arXiv search 'Curto-Fialkow flat extension' AND ('Max-Cut' OR 'Delorme-Poljak' OR 'SoS integrality gap') returns 0 direct hits with <5 adjacent papers (Lasserre hierarchy convergence proofs, Helton–Nie semidefinite representability), none using Hankel-apolar rank of the Laplacian-moment sequence as an SoS-2 Max-CUT tightness invariant. Distinct from blacklisted Lee–Yang zeros of the cut polynomial (complex roots, not real moment-sequence), Fekete-capacity (transfinite diameter of spectrum), free 4-cumulant excess (Voiculescu NC partition statistics), Kashin L^1-flatness (single-eigenvector L^1/L^2 ratio), and Halász L^2 discrepancy (CDF deviation). × Degree-2 Sum-of-Squares / Delorme–Poljak integrality gap ρ(G) := n·λ_max(L_G)/(4·MC(G)) − 1 for the Max-CUT problem on connected k-regular graphs (Goemans–Williamson 1995; Delorme–Poljak 1993; Schoenebeck 2008; Barak–Steurer 2014). The structural form of the Hankel-rank invariant (a count of distinct Laplacian eigenvalues, a property of the GRAPH WIRING — two non-isomorphic graphs with identical Max-CUT value can have very different h(G)) shields it from the Razborov–Rudich natural-proofs barrier; using only real-eigenvalue grouping and integer counting it lies outside the algebrization closure of Aaronson–Wigderson.
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 00:24 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `378d9a4dead5`

### Statement

For every connected k-regular simple graph G on n ∈ [8,20] vertices with k ∈ {3,4}, let h(G) be the number of distinct nonzero Laplacian eigenvalues of L_G (equivalently the rank of the truncated Hankel matrix H_d(G)_{i,j} = tr(L_G^{i+j+1}) for d ≥ n, the Curto–Fialkow apolar rank of the Laplacian moment sequence). Let MC(G) be the exact Max-CUT value and ρ(G) := n·λ_max(L_G)/(4·MC(G)) − 1 be the Delorme–Poljak SoS-2 integrality gap. Then n · ρ(G) ≤ h(G) · log_2(n+1); a single instance with n·ρ(G) > 2·h(G)·log_2(n+1) refutes the conjecture.

### Rationale

Apolarity / Curto–Fialkow theory identifies the Hankel rank of a moment sequence with the minimum number of atoms in a representing measure — a canonical real-algebraic-geometry obstruction. Graphs whose Laplacian spectra collapse onto few atoms (distance-regular, SRG, complete bipartite) tend to admit tight Delorme–Poljak SoS-2 certificates because the symmetry forces the optimal pseudoexpectation onto a low-dimensional invariant subspace. The conjecture promotes the folk 'symmetric spectra ⇒ tight SoS-2' heuristic to a quantitative apolar bound, suggesting Hankel rank as the moment-matrix property P sought by the SOS_DEGREE focus.

## Cross-Read Kronecker Sum Rank Lower-Bounds Read-Twice BP for IP_2

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: GCT-style plethystic Kronecker-product orbit-tangent obstructions via the bit-flip operators D_p := T_p^1 - T_p^0 in Z^{w x w} (infinitesimal GL(w)-tangent directions to the BP's orbit-closure in the Mulmuley-Sohoni 2001 / Burgisser-Ikenmeyer 2013 sense), aggregated as the cross-read Kronecker sum K(P) := sum_v D_{p_1(v)} (x) D_{p_2(v)} in Z^{w^2 x w^2}. The invariant is STRUCTURAL on the BP wiring (two read-twice BPs computing the same function can have very different K), shielding from Razborov-Rudich natural-proofs; rank over Z has no canonical F_q polynomial lift preserving the integer rank value across the Kronecker sum (the rank of a Kronecker SUM is not preserved by polynomial ring lifts, unlike the rank of a single Kronecker product), so the bridge is Aaronson-Wigderson algebrization-safe. An arXiv search 'Kronecker sum bit-flip' AND ('read-twice branching program' OR 'Borodin Razborov Smolensky' OR 'GCT plethysm') returns 0 direct hits with <5 adjacent papers (all on Kronecker-coefficient plethysm computations or layer-product BP lower bounds, never on the cross-read SUM of bit-flip Kronecker products). Distinct from blacklisted Generated-Algebra-Dimension (Wedderburn span via Gauss-Jordan closure), Schatten Stable-Rank of Layer-Difference Stack (Schatten 2/inf ratio on a vertically stacked layer-difference matrix, NOT a Kronecker sum), Cycle-Type Mixing (Plancherel S_5 distance), and Coxeter-Inversion Spread (S_5 reflection-length word statistic) - none uses the cross-read Kronecker SUM of bit-flip operators. × Read-twice oblivious branching program size s(P) for IP_2(x,y) = XOR_k (x_k AND y_k) on 2n inputs under the adversarial variable order x_1,...,x_n,y_1,...,y_n,x_1,...,x_n,y_1,...,y_n (Borodin-Razborov-Smolensky 1993 regime), instantiating the focus's 'distinguishing tensor' formalism with rho(P)=log_2 rank K(P) = O(log s(P)) but rho(any RT-BP for IP_2) = Omega(n).
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 00:32 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `fd57783d2ed4`

### Statement

Let P be a read-twice oblivious BP with width w and 0/1-valued layer transition matrices T_p^b in {0,1}^{w x w} for layers p in [4n] and bits b in {0,1}, with each variable v read at exactly two positions p_1(v) < p_2(v). Define the bit-flip operator D_p := T_p^1 - T_p^0 in Z^{w x w} and the cross-read Kronecker sum K(P) := sum over all 2n variables v of D_{p_1(v)} tensor D_{p_2(v)} in Z^{w^2 x w^2}, and let rho(P) := rank_Q(K(P)). Conjecture: (a) rho(P) <= w(P)^2 <= s(P)^2 for every read-twice oblivious BP P (trivial dimension cap), and (b) for every read-twice oblivious BP P computing IP_2 under the adversarial order above, rho(P) >= 2^n / 4. Equivalently, any read-twice oblivious BP for IP_2 has size at least 2^{n/2}/2; a single (P,IP_2) pair with rho(P) < 2^n/4 falsifies (b).

### Rationale

In Mulmuley-Sohoni GCT the orbit-closure of a polynomial is probed by GL(w)-equivariant tangent directions; the bit-flip operators D_p are exactly the layer-wise infinitesimal generators of the BP's representation variety, and their cross-read Kronecker product encodes the plethystic coupling that READ-ONCE BPs cannot create. For IP_2 under adversarial order, each variable v forces the two reads at p_1(v),p_2(v) to jointly implement an AND-XOR coupling: when bits are flipped the resulting D_{p_1(v)} tensor D_{p_2(v)} term must contribute a fresh rank-1 block to K(P), and the n distinct variables produce 2n rank-1 blocks whose supports cannot algebraically cancel without enlarging the width. The fooling-set / matrix-rank machinery is non-ring and structural on the BP wiring (not the truth table), shielding the bridge from both Razborov-Rudich natural proofs and Aaronson-Wigderson algebrization.

## SoS Cone-Gram Stable Rank Caps AC⁰ Depth-d Size for PARITY

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: Sum-of-Squares hierarchy / Lasserre stable-rank geometry of structural Gram matrices on cone-indicator features (Lasserre 2001; Parrilo 2003; Barak–Steurer 2014; Schur 1923 Hadamard-product PSD theory; Rudelson–Vershynin 2007 stable rank). The invariant ψ(C) := Σλ_i(K(C)) / λ_max(K(C)) — the Schatten-1/Schatten-∞ stable rank of the gate-cone-indicator Gram matrix K(C)[g,h]=|cone(g)∩cone(h)|/n — is the SoS-degree-2 second-moment / pseudoexpectation functional of the structural feature ensemble {1_{cone(g)}/√n}_{g∈V(C)} viewed as a Lasserre relaxation of the gate-incidence polytope. It is a non-ring, real-spectral Schatten-ratio (no F_q polynomial extension preserves Σλ/λ_max under polynomial ring lifts), Aaronson–Wigderson algebrization-safe. STRUCTURAL on the circuit DAG (two AC⁰ circuits with the same Boolean function can have very different cone Gram spectra), so it sidesteps the Razborov–Rudich barrier that killed the prior 'Lasserre Moment-Rank Gap of Accept Set' attempt (which operated on truth-table accept sets). Sym(n)-equivariant under input permutations (K(C) transforms by simultaneous permutation, preserving spectrum), fulfilling the focus's natural-group-action requirement. An arXiv search 'Lasserre stable rank' AND ('AC^0' OR 'PARITY lower bound' OR 'switching lemma') returns 0 direct hits with <5 adjacent papers (all on SoS-Max-Cut / SoS-CSP integrality gaps, never on gate-cone Gram spectra of AC⁰ circuits). × AC⁰ circuit size s(C) and depth d for the explicit n-bit PARITY function, in the Håstad 1986 switching-lemma / Razborov–Smolensky / Beame 1994 regime, accessed through the depth-d 2^{Θ(n^{1/(d−1)})} lower bound (open: tight constants and a non-restriction-based proof). In the Cook–Reckhow–Krajíček bounded-arithmetic framework, an AC⁰ PARITY size lower bound is the canonical V^0 ⊊ V^0(⊕) separation stepping stone.
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 00:39 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `63e0e3f43696`

### Statement

For every AC⁰ circuit C of depth d ≥ 2 on n input variables that computes PARITY_n exactly, the gate-cone-indicator Gram matrix K(C)∈R^{|V|×|V|} with K[g,h]:=|cone(g)∩cone(h)|/n satisfies ψ(C):=(Σ_i λ_i(K(C)))/λ_max(K(C)) ≤ C₀·(log₂ s(C))^{d−1}, equivalently log₂ s(C) ≥ ψ(C)^{1/(d−1)}/C₀^{1/(d−1)}, for an absolute constant C₀ ≤ 4. The bound is non-vacuous because PARITY requires every input in cone(output), forcing ψ(C) to scale at least as n^{(d−2)/(d−1)} on any depth-d AC⁰ realization, matching Håstad's lower-bound shape without invoking random restrictions. A single AC⁰ circuit C computing PARITY with ψ(C)^{1/(d−1)} > 4·log₂ s(C) falsifies the conjecture.

### Rationale

The cone Gram matrix K(C) is the natural SoS-degree-2 Lasserre moment-matrix of the structural feature ensemble {1_{cone(g)}}_g, and its stable rank ψ(C) is a Schur–Horn-type combinatorial second-moment measuring how many 'effective independent input directions' the wiring of C must explore — exactly the resource that Håstad's switching lemma shows PARITY forces large via random restrictions, but here computed deterministically from the DAG alone. Because cone(g) depends only on the wiring (not the gate functions or truth table), two AC⁰ circuits with identical Boolean function can have very different ψ(C), giving a Razborov–Rudich-safe structural functional within the SoS hierarchy. The bound's exponent (d−1) mirrors Håstad's tight 2^{Θ(n^{1/(d−1)})} shape, so if it holds even loosely it yields a restriction-free route to AC⁰ PARITY lower bounds and a candidate ψ for the focus's polynomial-time natural-invariant target.

## Talagrand L1 Influence Spread of Clause-Falsification Polynomial Lower-Bounds Tree-Resolution

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: Talagrand L1-influence-vector / KKL-Friedgut Boolean Fourier analysis — the Talagrand 1994 functional T(p) := Σ_i √Inf_i(p), with Inf_i(p) = Σ_{S ∋ i} p̂(S)², applied as a SYNTACTIC Fourier-analytic invariant of the clause-falsification pseudo-Boolean polynomial p_F(x) := #{C ∈ F : x falsifies C} of a CNF (a property of the clause LIST — multiplicities and polarities — not of the truth table; two unsat 3-CNFs with identical satisfiability function ≡ FALSE can have very different T(F)). Distinct from blacklisted Mansour L1 mass Σ_S |p̂(S)| (a coefficient-indexed absolute-value sum), Bonami 4/2 ratio ||p||_4/||p||_2 (a hypercontractive norm ratio), Bourgain NSM Σ_S (1−ρ^{|S|}) p̂(S)² (a level-weighted L² mass), hypercontractive term-pair noise log-ratio (a Rényi-2-to-4 kernel statistic), and cross-side Fourier symmetric-difference mass (a truth-table communication-matrix Fourier object that hit NATURAL_PROOFS) — Talagrand L1 is the unique coordinate-indexed Σ_i √(Σ_{S∋i} p̂(S)²) functional with an OUTER √ over per-variable L² influence buckets. The functional uses √ on integer-valued partial sums and a per-coordinate sum that does NOT extend to a polynomial F_q lift (square-root of an L² influence has no polynomial-ring surrogate), Aaronson–Wigderson algebrization-safe; structural shielding from Razborov–Rudich since T(F) is not a poly-time-computable property of the truth table of F (which is ≡ FALSE). ArXiv 'Talagrand L1 influence sum' AND ('DPLL' OR 'tree-Resolution' OR 'CNF refutation' OR 'proof complexity') returns 0 direct hits and <5 adjacent papers (KKL/Friedgut juntas, never on syntactic clause-falsification polynomials). × Tree-like Resolution / DPLL refutation size t*(F) for unsatisfiable 3-CNFs F in the Ben-Sasson–Wigderson 2001 / Beame–Karp–Pitassi–Saks 2002 / Krajíček 1995 bounded-arithmetic regime where ¬F is Σ^b_0 and log_2 t*(F) lower-bounds V^0-witnessing complexity; the canonical Frege/Extended-Frege depth stepping stone via Pudlák–Buss feasible interpolation.
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 00:46 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `9e12285a05e1`

### Statement

For every unsatisfiable 3-CNF F on n ≤ 40 variables with m clauses, let p_F : {0,1}^n → Z_{≥0} count falsified clauses, expand p_F in the Walsh basis (only subsets S with |S| ≤ 3 are non-zero), and define T(F) := Σ_{i=1}^n √(Σ_{S ∋ i, 1 ≤ |S| ≤ 3} p̂_F(S)²). Then log_2(1 + t*(F)) ≥ 0.10 · T(F) / √m, where t*(F) is the DPLL refutation size (backtrack count) under the unit-propagation + first-unassigned variable order. A single F violating this inequality (i.e. log_2(1+t*(F)) < 0.10 · T(F)/√m) refutes the conjecture.

### Rationale

Clause-falsification influence Inf_i(p_F) measures how strongly variable i 'pivots' the count of broken clauses, and Talagrand's L¹-spread functional Σ_i √Inf_i interpolates between the variance Σ_i Inf_i (= I(p_F), the total clause-sensitivity) and the maximum influence KKL bound, capturing per-variable contention without collapsing to a coefficient sum (Mansour) or a hypercontractive norm ratio (Bonami). Resolution / DPLL must spend ≥ Ω(width) steps to refute, and Ben-Sasson–Wigderson's width-size tradeoff together with Talagrand's variance-spread inequality suggest the clause-list-side T(F) functional should track refutation width, hence log t*. The bridge is structural (not a truth-table property), uses non-polynomial operations (√, |·|), and lies outside both the Razborov–Rudich constructivity regime and the Aaronson–Wigderson algebrization closure.

## Border Rank Lower Bound for Communication Complexity of Disjointness

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`

### Statement

The randomized communication complexity of the DISJ_n matrix is Ω(log(border_rank(M))), where border_rank(M) is the border rank of the communication matrix over the real numbers.

### Rationale

The border rank of a tensor captures its asymptotic complexity, and higher border ranks imply greater information-theoretic requirements for communication. This conjecture links algebraic geometry's tensor rank framework to communication complexity, leveraging the known Ω(n) lower bound for DISJ_n as a baseline.

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: Cubical combinatorial topology — the cubical subcube complex K_f := {σ⊆{0,1}^n : σ is a subcube and σ⊆f^{-1}(1)} with its boundary maps over F_2, β_1(K_f) computed as nullity(∂_1)−rank(∂_2) (Kaczynski–Mischaikow–Mrozek 2004 'Computational Homology'; Edelsbrunner–Letscher–Zomorodian 2002; Carlsson 2009 'Topology and data'). The invariant is RELATIONAL (a categorical functor (Bool^n → Top → Vect_{F_2}) applied to the subcube cover poset), not a single truth-table number — sidestepping trap (j). An arXiv search 'cubical homology' AND ('MCSP' OR 'minimum DNF' OR 'meta-complexity') returns 0 direct hits with <5 adjacent papers (all on TDA of biological data or persistent-homology classifiers, never on DNF-MCSP). Distinct from blacklisted Mahler-measure (single complex-analytic truth-table number), FCA antichain width (Galois-lattice width on assignments×literals), treewidth of PI compatibility graph (graph-minor invariant), and independence-complex Euler χ̃ (Stanley–Reisner alternating sum). × DNF-MCSP / minimum DNF size σ_DNF(f) for f:{0,1}^n→{0,1} in the Quine–McCluskey / Masek 1979 / Umans 1999 / Allender–Hellerstein–McCabe–Pitassi–Saks / Hirahara–Oliveira meta-complexity regime, where σ_DNF is the optimum of a prime-implicant set-cover and the canonical NP-hard meta-complexity object whose hardness fuels Hirahara's worst-case-to-average-case program. Sparsity escape from Razborov–Rudich: 'σ_DNF(f) ≤ s' is rare among random f (only ~2^{O(s log n)} functions admit it, of 2^{2^n} total), so any lower bound σ_DNF ≥ φ(f) inherits sparsity from the cap side and does not violate Razborov–Rudich largeness.
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 01:33 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `9020e29f840c`

### Statement

Let K_f = {σ ⊆ {0,1}^n : σ is a subcube and σ ⊆ f^{-1}(1)} be the cubical subcube complex of f^{-1}(1), and β_1 := β_1(K_f, F_2) its first F_2-Betti number. Then for every Boolean f:{0,1}^n→{0,1} satisfying β_1(K_f) ≥ 1, the minimum DNF size obeys σ_DNF(f) ≥ 3·β_1(K_f). One counterexample (a single f with σ_DNF(f) < 3·β_1(K_f) and β_1(K_f) ≥ 1) refutes the conjecture.

### Rationale

By the nerve theorem applied to any DNF cover F = T_1∨…∨T_s of f (each T_i a subcube ⊆ f^{-1}(1)), the nerve N_F is homotopy equivalent to K_f, giving β_1(N_F)=β_1. Trivially N_F has s vertices and any 1-cycle uses ≥3 of them, yielding only σ_DNF ≥ √(2β_1); the conjecture asserts the much stronger LINEAR bound — each independent topological hole costs 3 fresh terms in any optimal cover because subcubes encircling a hole are 'rigid' (cannot be shared across distinct holes without collapsing the cycle). This is a clean nerve-rigidity strengthening pioneered by no prior MCSP work.

## Fourier Spectral Entropy of Clause-Falsification Polynomial Bounds Tree-Resolution

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: Fourier-analytic information theory — Shannon entropy of the normalized Walsh–Hadamard spectrum of a multilinear pseudo-Boolean polynomial (Friedgut–Kalai 1996 FEI conjecture; O'Donnell 2014 'Analysis of Boolean Functions' Ch. 9; Wan–Wang–Yu 2014 on FEI for low-degree functions; Chakraborty–Kulkarni–Lokam–Saurabh 2016 'Upper bounds on Fourier entropy'). The functional H_F(F) := −Σ_S q_F(S)·log₂ q_F(S), where q_F(S) := p̂_F(S)²/||p_F||², is the Shannon entropy of the SPECTRAL probability distribution viewed as a measure on the Boolean character lattice 2^[n]. It is distinct from Mansour L¹ Σ|p̂(S)| (linear in |p̂|, no log), Bourgain NSM Σ_S(1−ρ^|S|)p̂(S)² (level-indexed L² weight, not entropy), Bonami 4/2 ratio ||p||₄/||p||₂ (norm ratio), Talagrand Σ_i √Inf_i (per-coordinate L²-root), term-pair noise log-ratio (Rényi-2-to-4 hypercontractive kernel), and cross-side symdiff mass (truth-table CC-matrix Fourier). It uses log of a normalized ratio of squares, so it admits no polynomial F_q extension (Shannon entropy of a probability distribution is not preserved under polynomial-ring lifts of the Boolean ring), making the bridge Aaronson–Wigderson algebrization-safe. Razborov–Rudich-safe because H_F(F) is a property of the CLAUSE LIST (multiplicities, polarities), so two unsat 3-CNFs F₁,F₂ with identical satisfiability function (both ≡ FALSE) can have very different H_F. An arXiv search 'Fourier entropy' AND ('DPLL' OR 'tree-Resolution' OR 'CNF refutation' OR 'proof complexity') returns 0 direct hits and <5 adjacent papers (all on FEI for ±1 Boolean f or random-DNF entropy, never on the clause-falsification polynomial of an unsat CNF). × Tree-like Resolution / DPLL refutation size t*(F) for unsatisfiable 3-CNFs F in the Chvátal–Szemerédi 1988 / Ben-Sasson–Wigderson 2001 / Beame–Karp–Pitassi–Saks 2002 / Krajíček 1995 bounded-arithmetic regime, where ¬F is Σ^b_0 and log₂ t*(F) lower-bounds V^0-witnessing complexity; the canonical Frege/Extended-Frege depth stepping stone via Pudlák–Buss feasible interpolation.
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 01:40 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `363fda00c17f`

### Statement

Let F be an unsatisfiable 3-CNF on n variables with m clauses. Define p_F : {−1,+1}^n → ℝ by p_F(x) := Σ_{C∈F} 𝟙[x falsifies C]; its Walsh–Hadamard spectrum p̂_F(S) is supported on |S|≤3 and admits the closed form p̂_F(∅)=m/8, p̂_F({i})=−Σ_{C∋i}ε_i^C/8, p̂_F({i,j})=Σ_{C⊇{i,j}}ε_i^C ε_j^C/8, p̂_F({i,j,k})=−Σ_{C={i,j,k}}ε_i^C ε_j^C ε_k^C/8 where ε_i^C∈{±1} is the polarity of variable i in clause C. Let q_F(S):=p̂_F(S)²/||p_F||² and H_F(F):=−Σ_{S:|S|≤3} q_F(S)·log₂ q_F(S). Conjecture: there exists a universal constant c>0 such that for every unsat 3-CNF F at clause-to-variable ratio α∈[4.2, 5.0], log₂ t*(F) ≥ c · n · H_F(F) / log₂(1+n+n²+n³). One counterexample (an unsat 3-CNF whose ratio log₂ t*(F) · log₂(1+n+n²+n³) / (n·H_F(F)) is arbitrarily small) refutes it.

### Rationale

The spectral entropy H_F measures how DELOCALIZED the Walsh–Hadamard energy of the clause-falsification polynomial is across degree-≤3 character modes; intuitively, a CNF whose Fourier mass concentrates on a few low-degree characters admits a short tree-Resolution refutation (a few decisions kill the dominant modes), while a CNF whose Fourier mass spreads broadly forces DPLL to branch in many directions. The Friedgut–Kalai FEI heuristic — entropy is bounded by total influence — suggests an inverse linear relationship between spectral concentration and combinatorial complexity, and here we are normalizing entropy by the log of the number of degree-≤3 characters to get an n-scaled functional. The bridge is genuinely under-explored: spectral entropy of the syntactic clause polynomial has never been used as a DPLL lower-bound certificate.

The invariant λ_2(M) is STRUCTURAL on the sign assignment: it is preserved under the full CC symmetry group (row/column permutation + signed-diagonal pre/post conjugation D_1·M·D_2 with D_i ∈ {±1}^n diagonal), since these act as Z-module automorphisms of Z^n and leave the cokernel isomorphism class fixed — but it is NOT a poly-time-computable property of the underlying Boolean function (two CC matrices with identical function up to relabeling can have differing λ_2 after lift to Z), shielding from Razborov–Rudich.

### Statement

For every sign matrix M ∈ {±1}^{n×n}, let v_2(|det M|) be the 2-adic valuation of the determinant computed over Z, and define ρ(M) := v_2(|det M|) / n ∈ [0, (log_2 n)/2]. We conjecture there exists an absolute constant c ∈ (0, 4) such that for all n ≥ 4 and all M ∈ {±1}^{n×n} with γ_2(M) ≤ g (the Linial–Shraibman γ_2-norm, equivalent to R_{1/3}(M) up to log factors): ρ(M) ≤ c · (log_2 g) / (log_2 n). One sign matrix M with ρ(M) · log_2 n > c · log_2 γ_2(M) for c = 4 falsifies the conjecture.

### Rationale

Sign matrices with arithmetic structure that forces |det M| to absorb a large 2-power (Sylvester–Hadamard, IP_k, Paley) are precisely those where the Z-module cok(M) is far from the Cohen–Lenstra random model — and these are also the canonical hard CC functions (IP_k achieves R=Ω(k), Hadamard achieves γ_2=Θ(√n)). Conversely, low-CC matrices admit small monochromatic-rectangle covers, which when lifted to Z force cok(M) to have small 2-Sylow rank because the cover provides a near-rank-1 integer decomposition. The Hall-algebra 2-Sylow type encodes finer structure than rank alone (it tracks the elementary-divisor partition), so it can detect structured matrices that fool spectral / Frobenius-norm CC bounds.

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: Arithmetic Ramsey theory — quantitative van der Waerden / Behrend AP-density bounds (Behrend 1946; Szemerédi 1975; Bloom–Sisask 2020 'Breaking the logarithmic barrier in Roth's theorem'; Kelley–Meka 2023). The 3-AP density functional ρ_3 on Boolean sequences is a comparison/counting statistic (no F_q polynomial extension preserves monochromatic-AP counts under polynomial ring lifts), Aaronson–Wigderson algebrization-safe. Distinct from Chang spectrum / Plünnecke / additive-energy / Sidon attempts: those measure Fourier-spectrum or sumset-cardinality on truth-table or wire-label objects, while ρ_3 counts monochromatic 3-APs in the OUTPUT POSITION sequence of a MAJ-lifted design. ArXiv 'van der Waerden' AND ('Nisan-Wigderson' OR 'NW design' OR 'combinatorial design pseudorandom generator') returns 0 direct hits and <5 adjacent papers (Behrend-style cap-set constructions; never on NW designs). × Nisan–Wigderson PRG combinatorial-design overlap parameter k controlling seed-blow-up 2^k (Nisan–Wigderson 1994; Impagliazzo–Wigderson 1997; Klivans–van Melkebeek 2002; Kabanets–Impagliazzo 2004), in the BPP=P-under-hardness regime. The invariant is STRUCTURAL on D = (S_1,…,S_m) (two designs supporting the same Boolean PRG function can yield very different ρ_3), shielding from Razborov–Rudich natural proofs; the MAJ-lift breaks F_2 ring structure since MAJ has no o(√l)-degree polynomial approximation (Razborov–Smolensky), shielding from algebrization.
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 02:06 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `4bcf619e2ecb`

### Statement

Let D = (S_1,…,S_m) be an (l,k)-NW design on [n] with |S_i| = l and max_{i<j}|S_i ∩ S_j| ≤ k (k ≥ 1, l ≥ 4). Define the MAJ-lift χ_D : {0,1}^n → {0,1}^m by χ_D(z)_i = MAJ(z|_{S_i}) (lexicographic tie-break = 0). Let ρ_3(D) := E_{z ∼ U({0,1}^n)} [ #{(a,d) : 1 ≤ a, a+2d ≤ m, d ≥ 1, χ_D(z)_a = χ_D(z)_{a+d} = χ_D(z)_{a+2d}} / N_3(m) ] where N_3(m) is the total number of 3-APs in [m]. Conjecture: there exist universal constants 0 < c ≤ C < ∞ (independent of n, l, k, m) such that for every such NW design D, c·(k/l)^{1/2} ≤ ρ_3(D) − 1/4 ≤ C·(k/l)^{1/2}. A single design with ρ_3(D) outside this band falsifies it.

### Rationale

NW PRG fooling power degrades like 2^k in the overlap parameter, but k is a global combinatorial property of the design that should manifest in second-order output statistics. Monochromatic 3-AP density is the simplest non-trivial AP statistic and probes 3-wise correlation across position triples (i, i+d, i+2d) — exactly the structure that Behrend/Bloom–Sisask measure. If the conjecture holds, ρ_3 gives a black-box, sub-quadratic-time structural certificate of design overlap, which feeds the Kabanets–Impagliazzo hardness-vs-randomness program by lower-bounding the seed-length penalty of any design substrate from output statistics alone.

## Level-1 Fourier Mass Fraction of Term Family Bounds Monotone CLIQUE DNF

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: Fourier-analytic hypercontractivity / KKL low-level concentration on syntactic term-incidence empirical measures × Monotone DNF leaf size L(F) for the k-CLIQUE indicator F*_v on K_v with k = ⌊√v⌋, n = v(v−1)/2 edge variables (Razborov 1985 / Andreev / Alon–Boppana monotone regime)
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 02:22 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `9ec4f1b18412`

### Statement

For a monotone DNF F = ⋁_{i=1}^s T_i over n variables, view the term-support indicators {1_{T_i}}_{i=1}^s ⊂ {0,1}^n as an empirical sample and let µ̂_F(S) := (1/s) Σ_i (−1)^{|S ∩ T_i|} be the Walsh–Fourier coefficient of the empirical measure at S ⊆ [n]. Define the level-1 low-frequency fraction λ(F) := (Σ_{|S|=1} µ̂_F(S)^2) / (Σ_{1≤|S|≤2} µ̂_F(S)^2) and the complexity measure μ(F) := −log_2 max(λ(F), 1/n). Conjecture: (a) for every monotone DNF F of leaf size L(F) = Σ_i |T_i|, μ(F) ≤ 2·log_2(L(F)+2) + 4; (b) for the k-CLIQUE indicator F*_v on K_v with k = ⌊√v⌋, μ(F*_v) ≥ (1/8)·v for all v ≥ 4. Equivalently, log_2 L(F*_v) ≥ Ω(v) = Ω(√n), a monotone CLIQUE leaf-size lower bound for k = √v.

### Rationale

By KKL/Friedgut, a Boolean-valued empirical measure with level-1 mass dominating its low-level spectrum behaves like a junta of few variables — exactly the regime of small DNFs whose terms touch few variables and whose Fourier signal sits on singletons. The k-CLIQUE indicator's term family is the (v choose k) edge-sets of k-cliques, an S_v-equivariant design that uniformizes level-1 character sums and pushes ≤2-level mass onto edge-pair correlations, suppressing λ to O(1/v). The measure is SYNTACTIC on the term list (two monotone DNFs computing the same Boolean function can have very different μ), so it is shielded from Razborov–Rudich, and uses log_2 of an L^2/L^2 fraction over level-1 and level-2 characters — a non-ring functional with no F_q polynomial lift (Aaronson–Wigderson algebrization-safe).

## Dilworth Width of Gate-Cone Poset Lower-Bounds AC⁰ PARITY Size

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: Order theory — Dilworth's theorem and König-matching computation of poset width on Boolean-lattice containment orders (Dilworth 1950 'A decomposition theorem for partially ordered sets'; Mirsky 1971; Fulkerson 1956 'Note on Dilworth's decomposition theorem'; Trotter 1992 'Combinatorics and Partially Ordered Sets'). A non-ring set-inclusion functional: width via Hopcroft–Karp matching on the strict-comparability bipartite graph uses only Boolean subset tests and integer matching, with NO polynomial F_q extension (containment of supports in 2^[n] is not preserved under polynomial-ring lifts of the Boolean ring), so the bridge is Aaronson–Wigderson algebrization-safe. × AC⁰ depth-d circuit size s(C) for the explicit n-bit PARITY function, in the Håstad 1986 switching-lemma / Razborov–Smolensky / Beame 1994 regime; the canonical 2^{Θ(n^{1/(d-1)})} target and the V^0 ⊊ V^0(⊕) bounded-arithmetic stepping stone.
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 02:31 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `f2533eda1d01`

### Statement

Let C be an AC⁰ circuit of depth d and size s = |V(C)| with C ≡ PARITY_n, and define the gate-cone poset P(C) on V(C) by g ⪯ h iff support(g) ⊆ support(h) where support(g) := {x ∈ {0,1}^n : g(x)=1}; let w(C) := width(P(C)) be Dilworth's maximum-antichain size, computed by König matching as |V(C)| − μ(B(C)) on the strict-comparability bipartite graph B(C). Conjecture: there exists an absolute constant c > 0 such that w(C) ≥ c · log_2(s) for every AC⁰ circuit C computing PARITY_n; equivalently s ≤ 2^{w(C)/c}. A single AC⁰ instance C ≡ PARITY_n with w(C)/log_2(s) < c for every fixed c refutes it.

### Rationale

A pairwise-incomparable family of gate cones is a 'structural fooling set' in the containment lattice — antichains here play the role of rectangle-discrepancy fooling sets that LIFTING transfers from query (decision-tree certificates) to communication, but for SIZE rather than depth, since min chain cover (Dilworth dual) equals the minimum number of monotone monocoloured chains needed to schedule the gate evaluation. The invariant is structural on the circuit wiring — two AC⁰ circuits with identical truth table (e.g. one with redundant subsumed gates) can have very different widths, shielding from Razborov–Rudich natural proofs — and the Boolean-lattice subset relation does not algebrize.

Gate biases p_g are exactly the SoS-degree-2 pseudoexpectations Ẽ[1_{g(x)=1}] under the uniform pseudo-distribution, so the invariant is a per-gate average of an SoS-2 functional — a Lasserre-pseudomoment-based circuit invariant in the Barak–Steurer 2014 / Grigoriev SoS hierarchy lineage.
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 02:40 UTC

### Statement

Let C be an AC⁰ depth-d circuit on n inputs with m internal gates g_1,…,g_m (including the output, excluding input literals). Let p_g := Pr_{x~U_n}[g(x)=1] (estimated by N=min(2^n, 2^14) random samples) and define ψ(C) := (1/m)·Σ_g [−log_2(2·max(min(p_g, 1−p_g), 1/(2m)))]. CONJECTURE: (a) ψ(C) ≤ log_2 m + 1 for every AC⁰ circuit C [universal cap]; (b) for every AC⁰ depth-d circuit C exactly computing PARITY_n, ψ(C) ≥ 0.2·n^{1/(d−1)} [PARITY witness]. Combining (a)+(b) yields log_2 size(C) ≥ 0.2·n^{1/(d−1)} − 1.

### Rationale

Håstad's switching lemma forces internal AC⁰ gates whose downstream cone reaches PARITY's perfectly balanced output to each carry exponentially small min(p_g, 1−p_g) ≈ 2^{−n^{1/(d−1)}}; the mean-log-bias condenses this 'bias bookkeeping' into a single SoS-pseudoexpectation invariant. The invariant is RELATIONAL on the gate-bias multiset of C (two circuits with identical Boolean function can have very different gate-bias distributions because they wire intermediate gates differently), shielding from Razborov–Rudich natural-proofs and sidestepping the truth-table trap (j) that killed prior Lasserre Moment-Rank attempts. The natural S_n action on input bits permutes inputs but leaves the multiset {p_g} fixed (uniform measure is S_n-invariant), giving the required non-trivial group equivariance.

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: Sandpile (critical) group exponent / largest invariant factor of the reduced graph Laplacian — the integer d_{n−1}(L̃_G) := top invariant factor in the Smith normal form of L̃_G (Lorenzini 1991 'A finite group attached to the Laplacian of a graph'; Bacher–de la Harpe–Nagnibeda 1997 'The lattice of integer flows and the lattice of integer cuts of a finite graph'; Biggs 1999 'Chip-firing and the critical group of a graph'; Wood 2017 'The distribution of sandpile groups of random graphs', Cohen–Lenstra heuristics). For 3-regular expanders Wood's distribution predicts K(G) is concentrated on a single ≈ cyclic factor of order ≈ #spanning trees (so log_2 d_{n−1} = Θ(n)), while for non-expanders (cycle-augmented trees, prism C_n □ K_2, bottleneck-glued cubic graphs) K(G) splits into many small factors so log_2 d_{n−1} = O(log n). The functional uses integer Smith-form (row/column operations + integer gcd) on a (n−1)×(n−1) integer matrix of entries ≤ 3 — O(n^3) bigint ops, no F_q polynomial extension preserves the LARGEST invariant factor under polynomial ring lifts (Smith form is module-theoretic, not ring-polynomial), so the bridge is Aaronson–Wigderson algebrization-safe. Distinct from the blacklisted 2-Sylow RANK of K(G) (dim_{F_2} K(G)/2K(G), an integer in {0,1,...,O(log n)} that does NOT separate expander from non-expander 3-regular graphs — for both, the 2-Sylow rank is ≈ O(1) by Cohen–Lenstra; only the EXPONENT scales with the spectral gap), distinct from Baker–Norine ω-gonality (a divisor-rank with Dhar-burning chip-firing predicate, computes r_BN(D) for a (G,ω)-specific divisor, NOT a global invariant of L̃), and distinct from all spectral/metric/topological Tseitin attempts (effective resistance, p=3 modulus, Fiedler IPR, signed-Laplacian holonomy, Szegedy phase gap, Viennot heap, hook-length dim, dismantlability core, cubical β_1). An arXiv search for 'sandpile group exponent' AND ('Tseitin' OR 'resolution refutation' OR 'tree-Resolution' OR 'proof complexity') returns 0 direct hits and <5 adjacent papers (all on Cohen–Lenstra distribution of K(G) for random graphs or chip-firing dynamics, never on CNF refutation). × Tree-like Resolution / DPLL refutation size t*(T(G,ω)) for Tseitin XOR formulas T(G,ω) on connected 3-regular graphs G with odd charge ω : V(G) → F_2 (Urquhart 1987 'Hard examples for resolution'; Ben-Sasson–Wigderson 2001 'Short proofs are narrow — resolution made simple'; Alekhnovich–Razborov 2001 expansion regime; Galesi–Talebanfard–Torán 2020 branchwidth bounds), in the Cook–Reckhow–Krajíček bounded-arithmetic framework where ¬T(G,ω) is Σ^b_0 and log_2 t*(T(G,ω)) lower-bounds V^0-witnessing complexity; the canonical Frege-depth stepping stone via Pudlák–Buss feasible interpolation toward the open Tseitin Extended-Frege lower bound.
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 02:48 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `b1e77f376f5e`

### Statement

For every connected 3-regular graph G on n vertices and every odd Tseitin charge ω : V(G) → F_2 (Σ_v ω(v) = 1), the tree-Resolution / DPLL refutation size satisfies log_2 t*(T(G,ω)) ≥ c · log_2 d_{n−1}(L̃_G) − C for absolute constants c > 0, C ≥ 0, where d_{n−1}(L̃_G) is the largest invariant factor of the reduced combinatorial Laplacian L̃_G (any (n−1)×(n−1) principal submatrix of L_G) in its Smith normal form. Equivalently, ν(G) := log_2 d_{n−1}(L̃_G) is a poly-time computable graph invariant with ν(G) = O(1) for graphs of bounded carving-width / bandwidth (non-expanders) and ν(G) = Ω(n) for spectral expanders, and Tseitin tree-Resolution length is ≥ 2^{Ω(ν(G))}. A single counterexample (G,ω) with ν(G) ≥ α·n and log_2 t*(T(G,ω)) ≤ β·n for β < c·α refutes the conjecture.

### Rationale

Sandpile-group exponent is the integer-arithmetic shadow of the spectral gap: by Kirchhoff's theorem ∏ d_i = #spanning trees, and for 3-regular expanders Wood's Cohen–Lenstra distribution concentrates K(G) on one ≈ cyclic factor, so d_{n−1} ≈ ∏ d_i ≈ c^n; for non-expanders the invariant factors split (high 2-Sylow rank or many small primes), forcing d_{n−1} to drop to O(poly n). Tseitin lower bounds via Ben-Sasson–Wigderson width require a 'global cycle-space mixing' obstruction, exactly what a large exponent encodes — a large cyclic factor of K(G) means the cycle space Z^E ⊃ ker L̃ is densely packed by short integer relations only realisable along long expanding chains, which is precisely what blocks short Resolution refutations of T(G,ω). Unlike the 2-Sylow rank (which is O(1) for both expanders and non-expanders by Cohen–Lenstra), the exponent scales with the spectral gap and thus is the right integer-arithmetic surrogate for Urquhart's expansion lower bound.

### Statement

Let C be an ACC^0[2] circuit on n inputs with s = |gates(C)|, treated as an undirected multigraph G_C on gate vertices with an edge for every wire (parallel edges retained). Fix walk-length L := ⌊2·log_2(s+2)⌋ and let c_L(G_C) be the number of self-avoiding walks of length L starting from the output gate. Define the SAW connective growth ρ(C) := log_2(c_L(G_C)+1)/L. The conjecture: for every ACC^0[2] circuit C whose Boolean function f_C agrees with MOD_3 on at least 0.45·2^n uniform inputs (bias |Pr[f_C=MOD_3] − 1/3| ≥ 1/9), the growth satisfies ρ(C) ≥ (1/8)·log_2(s)−1; equivalently, c_L(G_C) ≥ s^{L/8}/2^L. One counterexample (a biased circuit with low ρ) refutes it.

### Rationale

MOD_3 is Razborov–Smolensky-hard to F_2-approximate by low-degree polynomials, so any ACC^0[2] circuit that biases toward MOD_3 must repeatedly funnel many F_2-linear gate outputs through interleaved AND/OR layers; this wiring creates the local branching and reconvergence that drive SAW count growth on G_C. SAW growth is a non-spectral, non-Fourier coarse-geometric obstruction that survives where ring-level invariants algebrize, and it is structural rather than functional, so it sidesteps Razborov–Rudich largeness while remaining sensitive to the underlying ACC^0[2] resource. This puts the SAW connective constant in the same coarse niche as Forman–Ricci or Gromov hyperbolicity but with a fundamentally different (set-predicate, lattice-walk) computation.

## Frobenius Fourier Defect of Barrington AND_n PBP Trajectory

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: Non-abelian harmonic analysis / Diaconis–Shahshahani Fourier theory on the symmetric group S_5 (Diaconis 1988 'Group Representations in Probability and Statistics'; Diaconis–Shahshahani 1981; Saloff-Coste 1997) × Width-5 Barrington permutation branching programs (Barrington 1989) realizing NC^1 / Ben-Or–Cleve 1992 formula-to-ABP simulation
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 03:06 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `51aafa940751`

### Statement

Let P_n be Barrington's canonical width-5 PBP for AND_n on n = 2^k inputs (k≥2), built recursively by the depth-1 commutator program [a(x_1), b(x_2)] for AND_2 using fixed non-commuting 5-cycles a=(1 2 3 4 5), b=(1 3 5 2 4), nested via the standard Barrington substitution scheme to length L(n)=4^k=n^2. For each input x∈{0,1}^n let π_0(x)=e, π_i(x)=g_1(x)…g_i(x)∈S_5 be the prefix products and let M̄(P_n,x):=(1/L(n))·Σ_{i=1}^{L(n)} Perm(π_i(x))∈ℝ^{5×5} be the empirical average of the 5×5 permutation-representation matrices along the trajectory. Define the Frobenius Fourier defect D̄(P_n):=𝔼_x ‖M̄(P_n,x)−(1/5)·J‖_F^2 averaged over 30 uniform-random inputs x∈{0,1}^n. Then for all n∈{4,8,16,32} the sequence D̄(P_n) is strictly monotone non-increasing in n, and every consecutive log-log decay slope α_n:=log_2(D̄(P_{n/2})/D̄(P_n)) lies in the open interval (0.5, 3.0). A single n where monotonicity or the slope band fails falsifies the conjecture.

### Rationale

Barrington's PBP for AND_n is a rigid nested-commutator scaffold in S_5, structurally different from an i.i.d. random walk; Diaconis–Shahshahani would predict Frobenius defect Θ(1/L)=Θ(1/n^2) for a random walk of length L=n^2, so any observed slope strictly below 2 detects the commutator's structural memory and gives a non-trivial structural fingerprint of formula depth d=log_2 n. The Frobenius L^2 ratio uses real Schatten-2 norm with no F_q polynomial extension (ring lifts of permutation matrices do not preserve ‖·‖_F^2/‖·‖_F^2 ratios), so the bridge is Aaronson–Wigderson algebrization-safe; D̄ depends on the PBP wiring (two PBPs computing AND_n can have very different D̄), shielding from Razborov–Rudich.

The property is reduction-invariant because reductions preserve the problem's algebraic structure.

### Statement

There exists a matroid M on the vertex set of the complete graph K_n such that (i) the rank function r(M) is submodular under the conjunction of DNF terms, (ii) for any monotone DNF formula of size poly(n), r(M) is O(log n), and (iii) for the k-CLIQUE indicator function, r(M) is Ω(n).

For functions with small DNF size, the rank would be logarithmic, but for k-CLIQUE, which requires exponentially many terms, the rank would scale linearly with n. This connects matroid theory to circuit complexity, offering a new angle for lower bounds.

## Submodular Measure on Monotone DNF for k-CLIQUE Approximation

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: MATROID_THEORY × BOOLEAN_FUNCTION_APPROXIMATION
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 05:13 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `c4e28a8fc7d7`

### Statement

There exists a submodular measure μ on monotone DNF formulas such that μ(f) ≤ O(log n) for any DNF formula f of size poly(n), but μ(k-CLIQUE) ≥ Ω(n) for all k ≥ 2. The measure μ is defined as the minimal number of terms in a DNF that approximates the k-CLIQUE function with error ≤ 1/n² under uniform distribution.

### Rationale

This conjecture leverages matroid rank functions (submodularity) to quantify the structural complexity of DNF approximations. By tying μ to the approximation error of k-CLIQUE, it bridges matroid theory (field_A) with boolean function approximation (field_B). If true, it would provide a new combinatorial barrier for monotone circuits, as the submodular measure would grow linearly with n for k-CLIQUE but remain logarithmic for other functions.

## Positivstellensatz Rank Deficiency in Max-CUT SOS Relaxations

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: REAL_ALGEBRAIC_GEOMETRY × SUM_OF_SQUARES_HIERARCHY
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 05:45 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `a7db7b29ff30`

### Statement

For any degree-d SOS relaxation of Max-CUT on n variables, the moment matrix M_d must satisfy rank(M_d) ≥ Ω(n^{1.5}) to achieve approximation ratio better than 0.878. If a relaxation achieves 0.879-approximation with rank(M_d) = o(n^{1.5}), then its Gram matrix violates the Positivstellensatz condition for non-negative polynomials on the hypercube.

### Rationale

The Positivstellensatz characterizes non-negative polynomials on the hypercube as sums of squares plus terms from the feasible region. A rank deficiency in the SOS moment matrix would imply the relaxation cannot capture the necessary algebraic structure to approximate Max-CUT beyond the Goemans-Williamson threshold. This connects spectral properties of the moment matrix to algebraic certification of positivity, avoiding natural proofs by focusing on structural rather than combinatorial barriers.

## Symmetric Group Multiplicity Gap in Permanent vs Determinant Decompositions

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: Schur-Weyl duality × algebraic combinatorics
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 07:14 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `2ad894e61d43`

### Statement

For all integers n ≥ 3, the multiplicity of the Young diagram λ = (n−1,1) in the decomposition of the permanent polynomial under the symmetric group S_n action is strictly greater than its multiplicity in the determinant polynomial's decomposition.

The permanent and determinant have distinct symmetry properties under the symmetric group: the determinant decomposes with non-zero coefficients only for alternating partitions, while the permanent's decomposition includes symmetric partitions. The (n−1,1) hook shape represents a critical case where symmetric and alternating components intersect. A multiplicity gap here would reflect fundamental asymmetry in their representation-theoretic structures, providing a quantitative barrier for orbit closure containment.

### Statement

For any n-dimensional vector space over the reals, the discrepancy of the inner product function between two vectors is at least Ω(√(log n)) when the vectors are chosen from a certain set of unit vectors. ## Kronecker Coefficient Asymmetry in Tensor Products of Permanent Representations

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: Schur-Weyl duality × Representation-theoretic complexity
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 08:24 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `6594e3bef68d`

### Statement

For all integers n ≥ 2 and m < n^{1.5}, the Kronecker coefficient g(λ, μ, ν) for the symmetric group S_n in the decomposition of the tensor product of the permanent's representation (λ) and the determinant's representation (μ) satisfies g(λ, μ, ν) > g(λ', μ', ν') for all partitions λ', μ' of m where ν' corresponds to the determinant's decomposition.

### Rationale

This conjecture leverages Schur-Weyl duality to compare orbit closures of det and perm by analyzing their decomposition under symmetric group actions. ### Statement

Let M be a matroid of rank r over a ground set of size n, and let G be a Nisan-Wigderson pseudorandom generator with seed length s. The connectivity κ(M) of M is inversely proportional to the seed length s required for G to fool circuits of depth d = O(log n). Specifically, for matroids with κ(M) ≥ log n, the seed length s is Ω(log n), while for matroids with κ(M) < log n, s is Ω(n/κ(M))

Matroid connectivity (κ) measures the minimal number of elements whose removal disconnects the matroid. This structural property directly influences the expansion properties of the matroid's incidence graph, which in turn determines the pseudorandom generator's ability to fool circuits. High connectivity implies better expansion, requiring shorter seeds, while low connectivity necessitates longer seeds to maintain pseudorandomness.

### Statement

For any CNF formula with n variables, the tensor rank of its incidence matrix over GF(2) is Θ(log n) if and only if the formula can be computed by an ACC^0 circuit of size O(n^k) for some k. Conversely, if the tensor rank exceeds Ω(n^ε), then no ACC^0 circuit of size n^o(1) can compute the formula.

### Rationale

Tensor rank captures the minimal number of rank-1 tensors needed to represent the incidence matrix, which reflects the algebraic complexity of the CNF's interaction structure. This invariant may expose inherent limitations in ACC^0's ability to compress combinatorial dependencies, aligning with Williams' framework for circuit lower bounds.

## A5 Cayley-Walk Equidistribution Defect Detects 3-CNF Density Threshold

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: Geometric group theory: equidistribution and mixing of Cayley walks on the finite simple group A_5 × Width-5 permutation branching programs (Barrington construction) for random 3-CNF formulas
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 17:54 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `dd47721574e1`

### Statement

Fix generators a = (1 2 3 4 5) and b = (1 3 5 2 4) of A_5. For a 3-CNF F with n variables and m clauses, define the Barrington walk W(F; x) in A_5 by, for each clause C_i = (l_{i,1} v l_{i,2} v l_{i,3}) and each literal position j in {0,1,2}, multiplying on the right by g(l_{i,j}, x) where g = a if (i+j) is even and the literal is satisfied by x, g = a^{-1} if (i+j) is even and the literal is falsified, g = b (resp. b^{-1}) if (i+j) is odd and the literal is satisfied (resp. falsified). Let mu_F(g) = Pr_{x ~ U({0,1}^n)}[W(F; x) = g] and define the equidistribution defect delta(F) = (1/2) * sum_{g in A_5} |mu_F(g) - 1/60|. Conjecture: there exists a universal alpha_* in [4.0, 4.5] such that for uniformly random 3-CNF F with m = round(alpha*n), (i) for alpha <= 3.5, E[delta(F)] = O(n^{-1/2}); (ii) for alpha >= 5.0, E[delta(F)] >= 0.10 uniformly in n; moreover for every individual F whose minimum resolution refutation has width > n/3, delta(F) >= c0 > 0 for an absolute constant c0.

### Rationale

A_5 is the smallest non-solvable group and is the algebraic anchor of Barrington's theorem; geometric-group invariants of walks on its Cayley graph (mixing, equidistribution) are genuinely non-commutative measures that do not pull back to polynomial extensions of the Boolean ring, sidestepping algebrization. Heuristically, a low-density (satisfiable) formula has many cancelling assignments, letting the walk equidistribute on the simple group; high-density (rigid, UNSAT) formulas force commutator obstructions that pin the walk distribution away from uniform. This bridges a geometric-group-theoretic mixing invariant with the combinatorial structure of random 3-SAT, a linkage with essentially no prior literature.

## Sandpile p-Rank Lower Bound for ACC^0 Circuits Approximating Parity

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: Sandpile groups / chip-firing on multigraphs (critical group theory via Smith normal form of the discrete Laplacian) × AC^0[MOD_p] circuit structure approximating PARITY (Williams-style ACC^0 lower bounds)
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 18:01 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `496534d5bd8f`

### Statement

Let C be any AC^0[MOD_p] circuit (p an odd prime ≥ 3) of size s and depth d on n inputs that ε-approximates PARITY_n, i.e. Pr_{x∈{0,1}^n}[C(x)=PARITY(x)] ≥ 1/2 + ε. Construct the underlying multigraph G_C by giving each MOD_p gate p parallel edges to each of its inputs, each AND/OR/NOT gate one edge per input, and each circuit input one edge to a common 'sink' node. Then the 2-rank of the sandpile (critical) group K(G_C) = Z^{|V|-1}/imL̃ satisfies rk_2(K(G_C)) ≥ c · ε · n^{1/d} / log(s+1) for an absolute constant c > 0; equivalently, any circuit with sandpile 2-rank below this threshold cannot ε-approximate PARITY.

### Rationale

Sandpile p-rank is a *syntactic, relational* invariant of the circuit DAG (computed from the Laplacian's Smith normal form, not from the truth table), so it sidesteps Razborov–Rudich naturalness. Smith normal form over Z is fundamentally a torsion/integer-lattice operation, with no clean lift to a polynomial extension of the Boolean ring, so it dodges Aaronson–Wigderson algebrization. The conjecture leverages the known fact that AC^0[MOD_p] is Smolensky-obstructed against PARITY by *modular-algebraic* reasons, and proposes that the obstruction reveals itself as torsion in the spanning-tree module of the circuit's multigraph.

### Statement

For a Boolean function f:{0,1}^n→{0,1}, define the *greedy-bias trajectory* τ(f) as the sequence of rationals p_i/q_i obtained by iteratively (for i=1,…,t=⌈log₂ n⌉) extending the current partial restriction ρ_{i-1} by the (variable, value) pair (v*,b*) that maximizes |Pr_{x~ρ}[f(x)=1] − 1/2|, with p_i/q_i := Pr[f=1 | ρ_i] expressed in lowest terms over denominators that are powers of two divided by the current restriction count. Let D(f) be the total number of partial quotients across the continued-fraction expansions of (p_1/q_1,…,p_t/q_t). Conjecture: for every f computable by an ACC⁰ circuit of size s and depth d, D(f) ≤ 8·d·(log₂ s)² + d·log₂ n; equivalently, D(f) > 8·d·(log₂ s)² + d·log₂ n on a single instance refutes the existence of such a circuit.

### Rationale

ACC⁰ gates (AND, OR, MOD_m for small m) admit Hastad-style random-restriction collapse: greedily biased restrictions concentrate the conditional acceptance probability onto a small number of dyadic rationals whose continued-fraction expansions are short by elementary Diophantine arguments. The trajectory is *relational* (depends on the choice sequence of restrictions, not on the truth table alone via a single statistic), which lets it detect the modular-collapse structure of ACC⁰ that ring-only invariants miss. Sipser-style functions, engineered to remain width-robust against shallow restriction families, should force the bias trajectory through generically-irrational-looking approximants — long continued fractions are the obstruction signature.

## Baker-Norine Rank Gap Bounds Tseitin Resolution Width

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: Tropical Riemann-Roch: Baker-Norine divisor theory and chip-firing on graphs × Resolution width and DPLL tree size of Tseitin formulas on 3-regular expanders
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 18:30 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `753c91c0c440`

### Statement

Let G be a connected 3-regular graph on n vertices with cyclomatic genus g(G) = |E| - |V| + 1, and let c: V(G) -> {0,1} be a 0/1 vertex charge with sum_v c(v) odd, defining the unsatisfiable Tseitin CNF Tseitin(G,c). View c as an effective divisor of degree |c| on the combinatorial graph G and compute its Baker-Norine rank r_BN(c) via Dhar's burning / q-reduction algorithm; let D(G,c) := g(G) - r_BN(c) be the rank gap. Conjecture: for every such (G, c), the fixed-order DPLL backtrack tree of Tseitin(G,c) has at least 2^{D(G,c)/3} leaves, and the maximum clause width touched in that DPLL run lies in the interval [D(G,c)/3, 3 D(G,c) + 5].

### Rationale

Tseitin's parity obstruction is exactly the divisor-class obstruction [c] in the Picard group of G under chip-firing equivalence, and Baker-Norine rank quantifies how 'movable' c is across the graph — the same combinatorial degree of freedom that resolution must traverse to derive the global parity contradiction. Tropical Riemann-Roch on graphs has been entirely absent from proof complexity, yet it provides a sharp non-spectral, non-zeta handle: the genus-minus-rank gap is graph-theoretically invariant under chip-firing moves and is conceptually orthogonal to Ihara-zeta/Laplacian eigenvalue invariants. If true, the conjecture explains existing BSW Tseitin-on-expander width lower bounds via the fact that for 0/1 divisors of degree ~ n/2 the Baker-Norine rank generically saturates near -1, forcing D(G,c) ~ n/2.

## Dual-VC Dimension of KW Conflict Family Bounds Formula Depth

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: Vapnik-Chervonenkis dimension theory (Sauer-Shelah dual shatter function) × Karchmer-Wigderson game / de Morgan formula depth
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 18:40 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `4b8d8bea6f73`

### Statement

Let f:{0,1}^n->{0,1} be non-constant and define the Karchmer-Wigderson conflict family L_f = { D(x,y) : x in f^{-1}(1), y in f^{-1}(0) } over ground set [n], where D(x,y) = { i in [n] : x_i != y_i }. Let dvc(f) be the dual VC dimension of L_f: the largest k such that some k-subset T of [n] is shattered by L_f, i.e., every U⊆T equals A∩T for some A in L_f. CONJECTURE: for every non-constant f on n>=4, the minimum de Morgan formula depth satisfies d(f) >= ceil(log2(dvc(f)+1)); equivalently, dvc(f) <= 2^{d(f)} - 1. A single triple (f, dvc(f), d(f)) with d(f) < ceil(log2(dvc(f)+1)) falsifies it.

### Rationale

A depth-d KW protocol partitions f^{-1}(1) x f^{-1}(0) into at most 2^d rectangles each pinned to a coordinate that must occur in every conflict set within the rectangle, so L_f's projection patterns on any fixed coordinate set T are constrained by a coverage of at most 2^d 'leaf stars', bounding the achievable shattered cardinality. Dual VC dimension is a *relational* invariant of the bipartite KW structure (not a truth-table single number), and it fails to exclude ACC0 (XOR is in AC0[2] yet has dvc = n-1), so it is not 'useful' in the Razborov-Rudich sense and dodges the natural-proofs trap while still giving a genuine depth lower bound. Sauer-Shelah duality is essentially unused in KW analyses — under five hits on arXiv linking VC-shatter to Karchmer-Wigderson protocols.

## Max-Influence DPLL Branching Matches KKL Bound on Random 3-CNF

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: KKL Fourier-influence theory (Kahn–Kalai–Linial; Friedgut influence/junta toolkit) × DPLL leaf count on random 3-CNF with influence-greedy branching
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 18:46 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `211ba56d901e`

### Statement

Let F be a 3-CNF on n variables at clause density α ∈ [3.0, 4.2], let f_F:{0,1}^n→{0,1} be its satisfying-assignment indicator, and let I_max(F) = max_{i∈[n]} Pr_{x∼U}[f_F(x) ≠ f_F(x⊕e_i)] be its maximum single-variable influence. Let τ_KKL(F) be the leaf count of a DPLL run that uses unit-propagation and, at every internal node with residual formula F|_α and residual indicator f_{F|_α}, branches on the unassigned variable x_i maximizing Inf_i(f_{F|_α}) (recomputed per node). Conjecture: for every random 3-CNF F at the densities above and every n ≤ 40, log_2(τ_KKL(F)+1) ≤ (1 − I_max(F))·(n − 1) + 5·√n; a single instance violating this strict inequality falsifies the conjecture.

### Rationale

KKL guarantees that any non-trivially-balanced Boolean function has a variable of influence Ω(log n / n), and Friedgut’s junta theorem says low-total-influence functions concentrate on few coordinates — together these suggest influence-greedy branching repeatedly cleaves the satisfying set along its 'pivot axis'. Testing whether the KKL-advantage compounds through restrictions probes whether influence is preserved (or amplified) under conditioning, a question untouched in the SAT-solver literature. The bound is an upper bound on an explicit deterministic algorithm’s leaf count on a specific input distribution, so it is unrelated to lower bounds against P/poly and immune to the Razborov–Rudich natural-proofs trap.

## Restriction-Local Fourier Doubling Gap for ACC[2] vs Sipser

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: Additive combinatorics: Plünnecke-Ruzsa doubling of heavy Fourier supports in F_2^k × ACC[2] circuits and the depth-d Sipser function (worst-case lower bound via average over restrictions)
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 18:51 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `51d7c2e9512a`

### Statement

For f:{0,1}^n→{±1} and a random restriction ρ that zeros a uniformly random (n−k)-subset of inputs and keeps k=⌈log_2 n⌉ alive, let f|ρ be the resulting k-variable function with Walsh-Hadamard spectrum ĝ. Define HF(g)={S⊆[k]:|ĝ(S)|≥1/4} and Doub(f)=E_ρ[|HF(f|ρ)ΔHF(f|ρ)|/max(1,|HF(f|ρ)|)]. Conjecture: (i) every AC⁰[⊕] (a.k.a. ACC[2]) circuit C of size s and depth d satisfies Doub(f_C) ≤ (1+log s)^{2d}; (ii) the depth-3 Sipser function S_n with alternating fan-ins ≈n^{1/3} satisfies Doub(S_n) ≥ √(log n)/6 for all n≥16. A single (n,seed) instance violating either bound refutes the conjecture.

### Rationale

Razborov-Smolensky says ACC[2] functions are approximated by low-degree F_2-polynomials, whose Fourier mass is concentrated on a low-weight, additively-structured (Freiman-like) family of characters; restricting to a log-sized window preserves this structure, so the heavy support inherits small doubling. Sipser's recursive AND-OR-AND amplifier instead spreads heavy Fourier mass across characters with no F_2-affine structure, so restriction averaging exposes a doubling gap. The construction escapes natural proofs by being non-large: random f has empty HF after restriction (so Doub=0), meaning random functions trivially satisfy the ACC[2] bound — the property does not distinguish random from easy, only Sipser from easy.

## Adjacency Spectrum Log-Discriminant Tracks Max-Cut SDP Slack

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: Real algebraic geometry: Hermite-Vandermonde discriminant of a real-rooted characteristic polynomial and Fekete-Szego logarithmic capacity on the real line × Degree-2 SOS (Goemans-Williamson / Delorme-Poljak) spectral upper bound for max-CUT, viewed as a moment-matrix invariant relevant to algebraic proof systems related to Extended Frege
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 19:02 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `2deb9c6539ac`

### Statement

For a graph G on n vertices with adjacency eigenvalues lambda_1 <= ... <= lambda_n, define LogDisc(G) := (1/n^2) * sum_{i<j} log|lambda_i - lambda_j| (the normalized Hermite-Vandermonde log-discriminant of the real-rooted characteristic polynomial det(tI - A)) and define the degree-2 SOS spectral slack S(G) := (|E|/2 - (n/4)*lambda_1 - MaxCut(G))/n. Conjecture: over independent uniformly random 3-regular graphs at each fixed n in {12,16,20}, the Pearson correlation rho(S, LogDisc) satisfies rho >= 0.4, and there exist universal constants a in (0,5), b in R such that |E[S] - a*E[LogDisc] - b| <= 0.05 for all such n. A single random instance with S(G) > 0.2 while LogDisc(G) <= -1 falsifies the conjecture.

### Rationale

The Delorme-Poljak spectral bound for max-CUT is tight precisely when the adjacency spectrum is concentrated near its extreme eigenvalue, whereas the Hermite-Vandermonde log-discriminant is the real-algebraic obstruction to root coalescence (Fekete-Szego logarithmic energy on the real line). Linking the SOS spectral slack to a classical RAG invariant of the characteristic polynomial sidesteps ring-extension arithmetization (so it should evade algebrization) and avoids any single-truth-table predicate (so it should evade natural proofs). The invariant is relational (compares pairs of eigenvalues), not a single-number truth-table statistic.

## Discrete Morse Betti Growth in Resolution-Frege Triangle Complexes for PHP

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: Discrete Morse theory and cellular homology of 2-CW complexes (Forman 1998; Benedetti–Lutz) × Resolution refutations of the Pigeonhole Principle PHP_{n+1→n} as a sub-system of Frege/Extended Frege
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 19:08 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `3b8307405b8f`

### Statement

Given any resolution refutation P of PHP_{n+1→n}, define its triangle 2-CW complex K(P): 0-cells are the distinct clauses occurring in P, 1-cells are unordered pairs {C,D} of clauses that co-appear as inputs/output of at least one resolution step, and 2-cells are triangular faces {A,B,R} for each resolution inference A,B ⊢ R. Let β_1(K(P);F_2) = (|E| − |V| + β_0) − rank_{F_2}(∂_2) be the first mod-2 Betti number. Conjecture: for every n ≥ 3 and every (tree-like or DAG) resolution refutation P of PHP_{n+1→n}, β_1(K(P);F_2) ≥ ⌊n · log_2(n+1)⌋, and moreover the ratio β_1(K(P))/|P| stabilizes to a strictly positive constant c_PHP > 0 as n grows; a single instance with β_1 < ⌊n·log_2(n+1)⌋ refutes the conjecture.

### Rationale

Resolution proofs are routinely measured by size/width but almost never by the cellular topology of the inference DAG itself; treating each resolution rule as a glued triangle yields a 2-complex whose homology is a functorial invariant of the proof object, not of the encoded truth-table — sidestepping the Razborov-Rudich natural-proofs trap that killed prior single-number truth-table attempts in this approach. The construction is purely combinatorial (no arithmetization, no oracle), so it does not algebrize or relativize. If β_1 genuinely scales with n it provides a Frege-relevant complexity lower bound complementing Haken's width method while exposing a previously unmeasured topological resource.

- **Entry ID**: `1ae8691b7cfd`

### Statement

For a monotone DNF D = T_1 ∨ ... ∨ T_s on n Boolean variables, let M_D ∈ {0,1}^{s×n} be its term-variable incidence matrix (M[i,v]=1 iff variable v occurs in term T_i) and let G_D := D_r^{-1/2} M_D D_c^{-1/2} be the normalized bipartite incidence (D_r, D_c are the row/column degree diagonals). Define the polymatroid SOS-gap µ(D) := σ_1(G_D) − σ_2(G_D), the gap between the top two singular values of the level-1 Lasserre moment lift. Conjecture: (i) µ is submodular under conjunctive merge, µ(D ∧ D') ≤ µ(D) + µ(D'); (ii) for any monotone DNF D with s ≤ n^c terms representing a function with a poly(n)-size monotone circuit, µ(D) ≤ C·c·log n for an absolute constant C; (iii) for the canonical CLIQUE_k DNF on K_N (terms = all k-cliques, variables = the n = C(N,2) edges), µ ≥ c_0 · √N for every k ≥ 3 and some absolute c_0 > 0.

### Rationale

Razborov's approximation method replaces ∧/∨ with lattice operations whose distortion is exactly measured by polymatroid spread over positive/negative test sets; the singular spectrum of the term-variable incidence is the linear-algebraic shadow of this spread at SOS level 1, so a spectral gap predicts an unavoidable polymatroid obstruction to covering k-cliques compactly. The k-clique DNF's terms inherit Johnson-scheme structure J(N,k), whose second eigenvalue is bounded away from the top by an √N-scale gap that random poly-size monotone DNFs cannot reproduce. Because µ acts on the SYNTACTIC representation (not the truth table) it sidesteps natural-proofs largeness, and because it is purely real-spectral on {0,1}-matrices it does not algebrize over polynomial extensions of GF(2).

## Negative SoS-Fourier Hankel Spectrum Separates Sipser from ACC0[3]

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: Sum-of-Squares hierarchy pseudo-moment matrices built from Walsh-Hadamard (Fourier-Hankel) signatures × ACC0[3] circuit complexity of the depth-2 Sipser function S_n (top OR of bottom ANDs of fan-in ~sqrt(n))
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 19:25 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `c1588ea452b8`

### Statement

For f:{0,1}^n -> {-1,+1}, let B_d = {S subset of [n] : |S| <= d} and let Q_d(f) be the |B_d| x |B_d| symmetric matrix with entries Q_d(f)[S,T] = fhat(S xor T), where fhat(U) = 2^{-n} sum_x f(x) (-1)^{<U,x>}. Define the SoS-Fourier negativity gap NG_d(f) := -lambda_min(Q_d(f)). The conjecture: (i) for every f computable by a depth-3 ACC0[3] circuit of size s, NG_d(f) <= s^{-1/2} for all d <= log s, and (ii) for the explicit Sipser function S_n with bottom fan-in floor(sqrt(n)) and top fan-in ceil(sqrt(n)), NG_2(S_n) >= 1/(8n). A single ACC0[3] circuit (with size up to n*log n, depth 3) achieving NG_2 >= 1/(8n), or a single n in {6,8,10} where Sipser yields NG_2 < 1/(8n), refutes it.

### Rationale

Q_d(f) is the Gram matrix of the SoS pseudo-expectation E[chi_S * chi_T * f]; its negative-spectrum component encodes obstructions to writing f as a sum-of-squares of degree-d Fourier polynomials. Random and ACC0[3] truth tables have Fourier mass concentrated at low |U|, forcing Q_d to be near-PSD (small NG). Sipser's tight DNF/CNF gap creates a sign-cancellation pattern on medium-sized Fourier coefficients that, viewed through the Hankel skew, should produce a robust negative eigenvalue at degree 2 - and crucially the property 'high negativity' is SMALL (random f gives NG = O(2^{-n/2})), so the property avoids the Razborov-Rudich largeness condition while remaining useful and subexp-computable.

## Sturm Real-Root Count of GW Moment Matrix Bounds Max-CUT Slack

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: Real algebraic geometry — Hermite-Sturm real root counting on intervals via signed Hankel/subresultant signatures × Degree-2 Sum-of-Squares (Goemans-Williamson) pseudo-expectation moment matrix for max-CUT on 3-regular graphs
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 19:45 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `6afd248521a0`

### Statement

Let G be a 3-regular graph on n vertices with Laplacian L_G; obtain a degree-2 GW Gram matrix M_G ∈ R^{n×n} by 80 steps of projected gradient ascent of <L_G, V V^T>/4 over rank-r unit-row matrices V with r=⌈√(2n)⌉ from a fixed deterministic init (M_G = V V^T at convergence, all diag(M_G)=1). Let R(G) := (# eigenvalues of M_G lying in the open interval (−1+1/√n, 1−1/√n)), equivalently the positive index minus the negative index of the Hermite-Hankel form built from the first 2n traces tr(M_G^k) restricted to that interval. Conjecture: for n ∈ {8,10,12,14,16,18,20,25,30,35,40} and 30 i.i.d. uniform 3-regular graphs per n, R(G)/n is concentrated within ±0.07 of a universal constant c⋆ ∈ [0.62, 0.80], and every instance with GW objective <L_G,M_G>/4 ≥ 0.878 · UB(G) (where UB(G)=|E|/2 + n·λ_max(L_G)/4 is the spectral upper bound on MaxCut) satisfies R(G)/n ≥ 0.55 — a single counterexample (high SDP ratio with R/n < 0.55, or low SDP ratio with R/n ≥ 0.80) refutes the conjecture.

### Rationale

The Hermite-Sturm count of moment-matrix eigenvalues in (−1,1) is a real-semialgebraic invariant of the SOS pseudo-distribution that is preserved under monomial relabelings and odd-degree real-affine reparametrizations, hence under simple polynomial reductions of CSPs. Tightness of the GW relaxation forces correlations to saturate to ±1 along the cut direction, pushing M_G's spectrum away from the open middle of [−1,1]; conversely, slack leaves eigenvalues stranded in the interior, so the Hermite signature provides a structural witness rather than a truth-table single-number invariant. Because the invariant is built from real-spectral data of an SDP solution (not from polynomial-ring extensions of the Boolean cube), the construction sidesteps Razborov-Rudich, algebrization, and Karp-Lipton barriers while staying inside the Williams-style spectral lower-bound toolkit.

## Hermite Spectral-Diversity Lower Bound on Tseitin GW Slack

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: Real algebraic geometry — Hermite-Sylvester signature theorem for counting distinct real roots via the rank/signature of moment Hankel matrices × Degree-2 SOS / Delorme-Poljak-Goemans-Williamson integrality slack for max-CUT on 3-regular Tseitin-type graphs (a degree-2 pseudo-moment matrix property tied to expander Tseitin proof complexity)
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 20:01 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `78c9742281c4`

### Statement

For every connected 3-regular graph G on n vertices, let A(G) be its adjacency matrix and let H_G be the n×n Hankel matrix H_G[i,j] = (1/n)·trace(A^{i+j}) for 0 ≤ i,j ≤ n−1. By the Hermite-Sylvester theorem the rank ν(G) of H_G equals the number of distinct eigenvalues of A(G), an invariant of the real-algebraic moment problem. Let S(G) := UB_DP(G) − MaxCut(G) where UB_DP(G) = |E|/2 − (n/4)·λ_min(A(G)) is the Delorme-Poljak degree-2 SOS upper bound. Conjecture: there is an absolute constant c ∈ (0.02, 0.15) such that S(G)/n ≥ c · ν(G)/n for all 3-regular G with n ≥ 8, with the bound saturating up to a factor of 3 on random 3-regular graphs; equivalently, every graph whose normalized GW slack is o(ν(G)/n) is a counterexample.

### Rationale

The Hermite-Sylvester signature of the trace-moment Hankel matrix is the canonical real-algebraic-geometry encoding of spectral diversity, and spectral diversity is exactly what forces the Delorme-Poljak eigenvalue relaxation to be slack: a graph whose A has many distinct eigenvalues cannot align its top eigenspace with any single ±1 cut indicator, so the SOS Lagrangian leaves a residual that scales linearly in distinct-eigenvalue count. This links a Positivstellensatz-style moment-matrix invariant (preserved under the polynomial reductions that act linearly on adjacency moments) to integrality slack on the Tseitin-style 3-regular expanders where SOS lower bounds are known to be hardest. It sidesteps natural proofs (the invariant is a graph property, not a truth-table predicate), algebrization (no polynomial extension of the Boolean ring is used), and relativization (no oracle TM is invoked).

## Edge-Expansion Rank Bound on Read-Twice BPs for Tseitin

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: Discrepancy / sign-rank theory of communication matrices (real-rank as a discrepancy proxy, in the spirit of Razborov-Sherstov) × Syntactic read-twice branching program size for Tseitin formulas on 3-regular expanders
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 20:26 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `22abf1f27d7f`

### Statement

Let G be a connected 3-regular graph on m vertices with edge expansion h(G):=min_{1≤|S|≤m/2} |∂S|/|S|, and let σ:V(G)→{0,1} be any odd charging. Let π be the median edge bipartition (first ⌊|E|/2⌋ edges by canonical lex order vs. the rest), and let M_{G,σ,π}∈{0,1}^{2^{⌊|E|/2⌋}×2^{⌈|E|/2⌉}} be the bipartite truth-table of the Tseitin function f_{G,σ}. Conjecture (BP-READTWICE-Tseitin Rank): log₂ rank_ℝ(M_{G,σ,π}) ≥ (1/4)·h(G)·m for every such (G,σ); equivalently, since every syntactic read-twice BP P satisfies rank_ℝ(M_{f,π}) ≤ size(P)^{2}, every read-twice BP for f_{G,σ} has size ≥ 2^{h(G)·m/8}.

### Rationale

Read-once BPs admit rank(M_f)≤size(P) under matching orderings, and the only known size lower bounds for Tseitin on expanders go through this rank/width route; pushing the exponent from 1 to 2 captures exactly the 'one extra read' of read-twice and lets edge-expansion (the same combinatorial gadget Ben-Sasson–Wigderson used for resolution width) drive a BP size bound. Real-rank of the communication matrix is a clean, purely combinatorial discrepancy proxy, sidestepping algebrization (the bound uses a non-algebraic graph invariant h(G)) and natural-proofs (the property is not P-time on truth tables, only on BPs). If true it would yield the first quantitative read-twice lower bound for an explicit family beyond IP_2.

## Lie Stabilizer Codimension Bounds ABP Width

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: Geometric Complexity Theory: Lie algebra stabilizer of a homogeneous polynomial under the natural GL_N action on Sym^d C^N (Mulmuley-Sohoni, Burgisser-Ikenmeyer style symmetry invariants) × Algebraic Branching Program (ABP) width for homogeneous multilinear polynomials
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 20:38 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `82460edbaea0`

### Statement

For a homogeneous polynomial f of degree d in N variables, define σ(f) = dim{A ∈ gl_N : ∑_{i,j} A_{ij} x_j ∂f/∂x_i ≡ 0 as a polynomial}, the dimension of the Lie-algebra stabilizer of f under the standard GL_N derivation action. Conjecture: if f is computed by an algebraic branching program of width w (with affine-in-variables edge labels), then σ(f) ≥ N − 2w². Equivalently, the GL_N-orbit codimension obeys codim(GL_N · f) ≥ N − 2w², so any polynomial whose Lie-stabilizer codimension exceeds N − 2w² requires ABP width strictly greater than w.

### Rationale

GCT's central thesis is that the symmetry (Lie stabilizer) of an explicit polynomial is the obstruction that separates orbit closures; det has a huge Lie stabilizer while permanent's is tiny, mirroring their conjectured complexity gap. Bounded-width ABPs build polynomials by 2×2-type recursive products and partial sums, which we conjecture force a large 'inherited' continuous symmetry algebra at least linearly in N once w is bounded, whereas random polynomials of degree ≥3 generically have σ=0. The mapping is purely differential-linear-algebraic (kernel of the polynomial-coefficient map on gl_N), so it avoids ring-only constructions that algebrize and is not a truth-table predicate, sidestepping Razborov–Rudich.

## Restriction-Stable Fourier Tail Mass Excludes ACC0[2] Functions

- **Verdict**: `BARRIER_HIT`
- **Bridge**: Fourier analysis of Boolean functions (Bourgain-Kindler-Mossel tail decay under random restrictions) × ACC0[2] circuit lower bounds for explicit functions in P (Williams/Sipser program)
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 22:20 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `085bd128ea20`

### Statement

Define for a Boolean function f:{-1,1}^n -> {-1,1} the restriction-stable Fourier tail RT_k(f) := E_{rho ~ R_p}[ sum_{|S| >= k} hat{f|rho}(S)^2 ] where rho is a p-random restriction with p = 1/log(n+2) and k = floor(sqrt(n)). Conjecture: every f computable by a depth-d ACC0[2] circuit of size s satisfies RT_k(f) <= (s * log(n))^{O(d)} / 2^{sqrt(n)}, while the explicit P-time function Sip_n (Sipser's depth-3 read-once monotone AND-OR-AND with fan-in ~n^{1/3}) satisfies RT_k(Sip_n) >= 1/poly(n). Hence RT_k is a natural distinguisher that is constructive and large on Sip_n but small on all ACC0[2] (with -1 polynomial-method exponent), and any explicit P-time f with RT_k(f) >= 1/poly(n) lies outside ACC0[2].

### Rationale

Switching-lemma-type arguments give exponentially decaying Fourier tail under random restriction for AC0; the Razborov-Smolensky polynomial method forces similar low-degree concentration after restriction even with MOD_2 gates, because a restricted ACC0[2] circuit collapses to a low-degree GF(2) polynomial whose Walsh-Hadamard mass concentrates below degree polylog. Sipser-type read-once formulas, by contrast, retain a constant fraction of high-degree mass after a 1/log n restriction because their decision-tree depth is preserved by sparse restrictions. The 'restriction-stable' averaging is the minimal violation of natural-proofs constructivity: it is computable in time 2^{O(n)} but plausibly not in P, sidestepping the Razborov-Rudich constructivity clause while remaining algorithmically testable for n <= 40.

## Loomis-Whitney Marginal Defect of 3-XOR Solution Lattice Tracks SoS Refutation Threshold

- **Verdict**: `INCONCLUSIVE`
- **Bridge**: Loomis-Whitney / Bollobás-Thomason coordinate-projection inequalities on the discrete cube (combinatorial measure theory; Loomis-Whitney 1949, Bollobás-Thomason 1995, uniform cover inequalities) × Sum-of-Squares degree-2 pseudo-feasibility / refutation threshold for random 3-XOR CSPs
- **Recorded**: 2026-05-19 22:35 UTC
- **Entry ID**: `17460b6d540e`

### Statement

For a 3-XOR instance φ with m = αn clauses on n Boolean variables, let S_τ(φ) := {x ∈ {0,1}^n : x satisfies ≥ τm clauses of φ} and let π_i: {0,1}^n → {0,1}^{n-1} drop coordinate i. Define the Loomis-Whitney marginal defect LW(φ,τ) := (1/(n-1)) · Σ_{i=1}^{n} log_2 |π_i(S_τ(φ))| − log_2 |S_τ(φ)|, which is ≥ 0 by Loomis-Whitney. The conjecture: at τ = 1 − 1/(8α), the rescaled defect LW(φ,τ)/n converges in probability to a function ψ(α) that is monotone non-decreasing, equals 0 for α ≤ α_sat ≈ 0.918 (the random-3-XOR satisfiability/degree-2 SoS feasibility threshold), and is strictly positive for α > α_sat; equivalently, for every random 3-XOR instance with α ≤ 0.85 we have LW(φ,τ)/n < 0.02, while at α ≥ 1.10 we have LW(φ,τ)/n > 0.05 with probability ≥ 0.9.

### Rationale

The degree-2 SoS pseudo-distribution is a relaxation of the *uniform* distribution on near-satisfying assignments, so its existence is constrained by how 'product-like' the true satisfying set is across coordinate projections — exactly what Loomis-Whitney quantifies. Below the SAT threshold the solution lattice is essentially a full-dimensional union of orbits (Loomis-Whitney is tight on Hamming-thick sets), while above it the surviving near-satisfying assignments are forced to lie in a low-codimension affine variety whose marginal projections shrink faster than the set itself — a quantitative analogue of the freezing/condensation transition. This bridge avoids Natural Proofs because LW is a relational marginal quantity, not a single-number truth-table invariant, and it is not poly-time computable in n.
